=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given three integers n, m, k. A good array arr of size n is defined as follows:

Each element in arr is in the inclusive range [1, m].
Exactly k indices i (where 1 <= i < n) satisfy the condition arr[i - 1] == arr[i].

Return the number of good arrays that can be formed.
Since the answer may be very large, return it modulo 10^9 + 7.
 
Example 1:

Input: n = 3, m = 2, k = 1
Output: 4
Explanation:

There are 4 good arrays. They are [1, 1, 2], [1, 2, 2], [2, 1, 1] and [2, 2, 1].
Hence, the answer is 4.


Example 2:

Input: n = 4, m = 2, k = 2
Output: 6
Explanation:

The good arrays are [1, 1, 1, 2], [1, 1, 2, 2], [1, 2, 2, 2], [2, 1, 1, 1], [2, 2, 1, 1] and [2, 2, 2, 1].
Hence, the answer is 6.


Example 3:

Input: n = 5, m = 2, k = 0
Output: 2
Explanation:

The good arrays are [1, 2, 1, 2, 1] and [2, 1, 2, 1, 2]. Hence, the answer is 2.


 
Constraints:

1 <= n <= 10^5
1 <= m <= 10^5
0 <= k <= n - 1

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def countGoodArrays(self, n: int, m: int, k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

MOD = 10**9+7
class Solution:
    def countGoodArrays(self, n:int, m:int, k:int)->int:
        r = n - k
        # compute C(n-1, r-1)
        # factorial up to n
        fact = [1]*(n+1)
        for i in range(1,n+1):
            fact[i]=fact[i-1]*i%MOD
        invfact = [1]*(n+1)
        invfact[n]=pow(fact[n], MOD-2, MOD)
        for i in range(n,0,-1):
            invfact[i-1]=invfact[i]*i%MOD
        comb = fact[n-1]*invfact[r-1]%MOD*invfact[n-r]%MOD
        ans = comb * m % MOD * pow(m-1, r-1, MOD) % MOD
        return ans

class Solution:
    def countGoodArrays(self, n: int, m: int, k: int) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        runs = n - k
        max_fact = n
        fact = [1] * (max_fact + 1)
        for i in range(1, max_fact + 1):
            fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD
        invfact = [1] * (max_fact + 1)
        invfact[max_fact] = pow(fact[max_fact], MOD-2, MOD)
        for i in range(max_fact, 0, -1):
            invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD
        ways_runs = fact[n-1] * invfact[runs-1] % MOD * invfact[n-runs] % MOD
        return ways_runs * m % MOD * pow(m-1, runs-1, MOD) % MOD

Let's final code:

```python
class Solution:
    def countGoodArrays(self, n: int, m: int, k: int) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        runs = n - k

        fact = [1] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            fact[i] = fact[i - 1] * i % MOD

        invfact = [1] * (n + 1)
        invfact[n] = pow(fact[n], MOD - 2, MOD)
        for i in range(n, 0, -1):
            invfact[i - 1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

        ways_runs = fact[n - 1] * invfact[runs - 1] % MOD * invfact[n - runs] % MOD
        return ways_runs * m % MOD * pow(m - 1, runs - 1, MOD) % MOD
```

class Solution:
    def countGoodArrays(self, n: int, m: int, k: int) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        fact = [1] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            fact[i] = fact[i - 1] * i % MOD
        invfact = [1] * (n + 1)
        invfact[n] = pow(fact[n], MOD - 2, MOD)
        for i in range(n, 0, -1):
            invfact[i - 1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

        comb = fact[n - 1] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n - 1 - k] % MOD
        return comb * m % MOD * pow(m - 1, n - 1 - k, MOD) % MOD

Alternative O(min(k, n-1-k)) combination:
def comb(n, k):
    if k < 0 or k > n: return 0
    k = min(k, n-k)
    res = 1
    for i in range(1, k+1):
        res = res * (n - k + i) % MOD * pow(i, MOD-2, MOD) % MOD
But pow(i, MOD-2) each iteration O(log MOD) too slow if k=1e5? 1e5*30 ~3e6 okay maybe but can precompute inverses or use factorial. Factorial O(n) with one pow is better.

Let's maybe think if there is any issue with "n=6" and "m=1,k=3" ans=0. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is any issue with "n=6" and "m=1,k=2" ans=0. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is any issue with "n=6" and "m=1,k=1" ans=0. Good.

Let's maybe
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def countGoodArrays(self, n: int, m: int, k: int) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7

        fact = [1] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            fact[i] = fact[i - 1] * i % MOD

        invfact = [1] * (n + 1)
        invfact[n] = pow(fact[n], MOD - 2, MOD)
        for i in range(n, 0, -1):
            invfact[i - 1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

        # Choose which k adjacent pairs are equal.
        # The remaining n-1-k adjacent pairs must be different.
        comb = fact[n - 1] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n - 1 - k] % MOD

        return comb * m % MOD * pow(m - 1, n - 1 - k, MOD) % MOD
```
